- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Napoleon, leader of France (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: France
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Musketeer, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Unit: SPAD S.VII, Replacing Triplane (Requires technology: Flight)
        - Unique Building: Salon, Replacing Gallery (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Improvement: Chateau (Can only be built adjacent to a Luxury resource, and cannot be adjacent to another Chateau. +50% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains Fortifications, allowing owned Units to stay stationed in this tile after attacking.); Requires technology: Chivalry
        - Civilization Ability: Melee and Gunpowder Units may enlist defeated enemies, which do not consume Military Supply. +1 Culture and Tourism in every City for every 10 Military Units. +15% Military Supply from Population.
      - Leader: Napoleon
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Atomic Theory: Reveals Uranium and allows you to start working on the Manhattan Project, setting the stage for the production of nuclear weapons.
        Completing it would unlock: Computers, Nuclear Fission
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with game speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Third Alternative (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Third Alternative: Quantity of Strategic Resources produced (including Paper) is increased by 100%. Reduces Unit Gold Maintenance costs by 25%.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Rationalism (New Branch):
        - Rationalism focuses on maximizing Growth and Science output.
        - Adopting Rationalism grants:
        - * +2 Production and +3 Science from all Strategic Resources.
        - * +5% Science in all Cities.
        - Each Rationalism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +2% Science.
        - Adopting all Policies in Rationalism grants:
        - * Unlocks building Bletchley Park.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +12 City Security in every City.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Scientists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 6
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 8
        - Boldness: 10
        - WarBias: 10
        - HostileBias: 10
        - WarmongerHate: 1

        - GuardedBias: 8

        - WorkWithWillingness: 8
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 10
        - Loyalty: 10
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 8
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 6
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 7
        - MinorCivWarBias: 9
        - DenounceWillingness: 9
        - Forgiveness: 6
        - Meanness: 8
        - Neediness: 4
        - Chattiness: 2
        - DeceptiveBias: 10
        - Rationale: The current aggressive, expansionist persona is working. The Inca's last two cities are about to fall—Huamanga at 54% health with heavy bombardment and Vilcabamba. Vientiane (Siam's last city) at 31% health is also about to be captured. Florence, an Assyrian-allied city-state, is at 94% health and should be captured next after Siam and Inca. Germany remains our primary long-term enemy, but our peace treaty has 9 turns left. We must maintain high HostileBias and WarBias against these immediate targets and keep high MinorCivWarBias to capture Florence. Keep DeceptiveBias high to mislead Germany about our intentions after the peace ends. Loyalty remains high to keep our allied city-states (Edinburgh, Heidabir, Okilis, Ouagadougou) stable. Low Chattiness to avoid distractions.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Atomic Theory
        - Rationale: After Mobile Tactics completes, Atomic Theory will unlock Uranium and the Manhattan Project, paving the way for nuclear weapons. This is crucial for breaking Germany's heavily defended coastal capitals (Berlin and Beijing) in the upcoming war, complementing our conventional air and naval superiority.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Harappa
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: We have made peace with Harappa (city-state) after events. Resetting to neutral to avoid unnecessary conflict and focus on finishing Japan and preparing for Inca. This frees our military from a secondary front.
        - UpdatedTurn: 173
        
        ### Valletta
        - Public: 0

        - Rationale: Valletta (city-state) is at war with us but has no strategic value; resetting to neutral to avoid unnecessary conflict bias. Our focus is on major civs and key city-states like Edinburgh and Heidabir.

        ### China

        ### Indonesia

We need to eliminate them entirely to secure our control over their capital Jakarta (already held) and remove a distraction. Setting maximum hostility ensures our AI prioritizes capturing Samarinda and treats Indonesia as a conquest target, rather than maintaining a precarious vassal relationship that could flip.

Other flavors sustain our overwhelming production, gold, and science advantages.

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 44
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 10 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - World Religion: Catholicism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Napoleon, leader of France (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: France
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Musketeer, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Unit: SPAD S.VII, Replacing Triplane (Requires technology: Flight)
        - Unique Building: Salon, Replacing Gallery (Requires technology: Architecture)
        - Unique Improvement: Chateau (Can only be built adjacent to a Luxury resource, and cannot be adjacent to another Chateau. +50% Defensive Strength for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains Fortifications, allowing owned Units to stay stationed in this tile after attacking.); Requires technology: Chivalry
        - Civilization Ability: Melee and Gunpowder Units may enlist defeated enemies, which do not consume Military Supply. +1 Culture and Tourism in every City for every 10 Military Units. +15% Military Supply from Population.
      - Leader: Napoleon
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Atomic Theory: Reveals Uranium and allows you to start working on the Manhattan Project, setting the stage for the production of nuclear weapons.
        Completing it would unlock: Computers, Nuclear Fission
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with game speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Third Alternative (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Third Alternative: Quantity of Strategic Resources produced (including Paper) is increased by 100%. Reduces Unit Gold Maintenance costs by 25%.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Fraternity (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +3 Food in every City.
        - * +3 Science from City Connections.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Rationalism (New Branch):
        - Rationalism focuses on maximizing Growth and Science output.
        - Adopting Rationalism grants:
        - * +2 Production and +3 Science from all Strategic Resources.
        - * +5% Science in all Cities.
        - Each Rationalism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +2% Science.
        - Adopting all Policies in Rationalism grants:
        - * Unlocks building Bletchley Park.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +12 City Security in every City.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Scientists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #0:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - TargetID: 0
        - Rationale: Germany is our primary adversary. The peace treaty expires in 3 turns. We must prepare for a massive war and launch a full-scale offensive as soon as the treaty ends.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Rationale: Assyria is our active war target. Their military is extremely weak (2k strength). We must capture their western coastal cities (Nineveh, Nimrud) with our western navy and use them as a base to capture their inland capital Assur before Germany does.
        - Private: -100
        - Public: -100
        - TargetID: 2
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Rationale: Crucial to reveal Uranium and build the Manhattan Project. This will allow us to produce nuclear weapons to break Germany's heavily defended coastal cities and massive army.
        - Technology: Atomic Theory
    - #3:

      - args:
        - Rationale: Double strategic resources will immediately resolve our Oil deficit (-2 Oil) caused by our large submarine and tank forces, and provide ample Uranium and Aluminum for our upcoming war with Germany.

Increase Defense and CityDefense to 100 to withstand Germany's massive 34k army and their air force in Batam. Set Nuke and UseNuke to 100 to prioritize nuclear capability as soon as we research Atomic Theory. Increase Espionage to 100 to secure city-state alliances and defend our cities against German spies.
  - tokens:
    - inputTokens: 75305
    - reasoningTokens: 15015
    - outputTokens: 359
  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Strategic Situation**
            
            I'm currently assessing our progress on Turn 412 as France. My focus is on the strategic picture, evaluating our position and making high-level decisions to advance our objectives.
            
            **Conquering Key Capitals**
            
            My current analysis reveals we've secured five original capitals, but victory hinges on capturing Assyria's Assur and Germany's Berlin and Beijing. The German peace treaty expires in three turns, so I'm prioritizing actions against Assyria while strategizing for the German confrontation.
            
            **Bombarding Enemy Cities**
            
            My immediate focus is on Assyria, as we're actively bombarding their cities and closing in on Florence. I'm reassessing their city locations and my unit placements, realizing Halab is much further than initially perceived.
            
            **Revising Offensive Strategy**
            
            I've re-evaluated the geographic distribution of Assyria's cities. It appears Halab is significantly further east than initially perceived, requiring a shift in my offensive approach. My attention is now more focused on consolidating our western gains before committing fully to the eastern front.
            
            **Evaluating Sanctions Impact**
            
            I'm now analyzing the implications of the "Sanction: France" resolution. It appears to block new trade routes, deals, and corporation franchises, though some existing CS trade routes might be unaffected. I'm investigating how this specifically impacts our current economic situation and global standing.
            
            **Exploring Cultural Victory**
            
            My thoughts are shifting towards a potential Cultural Victory. I've noted the completion of Vilcas, and with a new caravan trained, I'm considering its destination. The "Passport System" adoption is on my radar for its tourism benefits. I’m realizing we are close to achieving the necessary influence with the remaining civilizations to win this way, despite current wars.
            
            **Choosing Next Policy**
            
            I am currently reviewing available policies with 19 turns until the next adoption. My focus is on selecting the most impactful policy, weighing options like "Total War" for production and warscore benefits against "Martial Spirit" for combat strength and reduced weariness, considering our existing Autocracy policies.
            
            **Pursuing Cultural Victory**
            
            My current focus is on solidifying our path to a Cultural Victory. Given our ongoing conquests, adopting "Futurism" now offers significant tourism bonuses upon capturing cities, which will be crucial for achieving influence with Germany and Assyria, especially as we're nearing their capitals.
            
            **Researching Strategic Tech**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our technology path. While Atomic Theory seemed optimal for nukes against Germany's formidable army and defenses, I'm questioning its immediate relevance versus other options and need to confirm our research focus after the last turn's adjustment.
            
            **Defending Against Air Assault**
            
            I'm urgently re-evaluating Germany's overwhelming air power, especially the massive force in Batam, just three turns before our peace treaty expires. Their air superiority is a severe threat to Tiwanaku and Surabaya, and I'm assessing our meager anti-air defenses to formulate a desperate counter.
            
            **Acquiring Nuclear Capability**
            
            My immediate priority is securing nuclear capability. Germany possesses Uranium and Heavy Bombers, posing a significant threat. I need to rush Atomic Theory to acquire Uranium and potentially build the Manhattan Project to counter their nuclear advantage.
            
            **Researching Nuclear Advantage**
            
            I'm confirming our research path is locked onto Atomic Theory, the necessary step to counter Germany's atomic threat and secure Uranium. I'm also reviewing World Congress outcomes, noting the "Sanction: France" enactment, and seeing the "Passport System" as a potential proposal.
            
            **Securing Diplomatic Majority**
            
            I've confirmed we possess enough delegates to pass the "Passport System" and boost our Cultural Victory chances. However, a recent event removed our alliances with three key city-states, lowering our friendship levels, which requires immediate attention.
            
            **Restoring Diplomatic Ties**
            
            I'm focused on understanding the sudden loss of alliances with key city-states. While the "United Nations" resolution seems to be the cause, I've noted the immediate re-establishment of an alliance with Okilis through a diplomat's actions. I'm investigating if this can be replicated.
            
            **Restoring Diplomatic Ties**
            
            I'm digging into why our friendships with Sofia, Okilis, and Ouagadougou plummeted. It appears the founding of the United Nations, proposed by us, inadvertently reset our influence with them, though Edinburgh's alliance remained intact for reasons I'm still investigating. Fortunately, I've already re-established an alliance with Okilis.
            
            **Investigating Diplomatic Fallout**
            
            I'm thoroughly reviewing the sequence of events surrounding the sudden loss of alliances. The United Nations resolution, which I proposed, appears to have drastically reduced our friendship levels with Sofia, Okilis, and Ouagadougou to exactly 50, from much higher values. I'm still deciphering the precise game mechanic responsible for this specific influence drop.
            
            **Re-establishing Alliances**
            
            I'm actively working to re-establish alliances with Sofia, Okilis, and Ouagadougou after our proposed United Nations resolution inadvertently lowered our friendship levels. Diplomats are en route, and I've already secured a new alliance with Okilis, confirming our ability to restore these vital ties.
            
            **Capturing Near City**
            
            My immediate focus is on Florence, which is critically low on health at 5%. With tanks and infantry positioned adjacent to it, I anticipate its capture next turn. This will remove a hostile city-state, and I've noted its alliance with Assyria.
            
            **Continuing War Effort**
            
            I'm also reassessing our position against Assyria. My units are approaching Halab, and I'm planning our next moves, considering our tank's progress into Florence's territory.
            
            **Analyzing Assyrian Front**
            
            My current assessment reveals a critical juncture against Assyria. While Florence is almost ours and Halab is within reach, I've realized Assur, their capital, is far west and currently beyond our direct reach, complicating domination victory plans.
            
            **Navigating to Assur**
            
            I'm analyzing our naval position. We have a strong fleet of 8-10 destroyers and submarines near Troyes/Marseille, capable of sailing west towards Lisbon and Assur. This fleet is positioned to attack Assur, which is surprisingly close at only 20 tiles west.
            
            **Assessing German Threat**
            
            I'm reassessing our strategic position concerning Germany, particularly their sphere of influence over key western city-states. This complicates our plans against Assyria, as engaging Germany will draw these city-states into the conflict, creating a large, multi-front battlefield in the west. I'm also now analyzing Germany's naval deployments.
            
            **Assessing German Border Situation**
            
            The sheer scale of Germany's western cities, particularly Beijing and Macau near our core territories, is alarming. This proximity significantly complicates any plans to engage them, given their extensive empire and potential to draw in allied city-states.
            
            **Analyzing Two-Front War**
            
            I'm currently weighing the immediate threat from Germany against the weakened state of Assyria. Germany's air force in Batam is a significant concern, especially as our peace treaty nears its end. They are also at war with Assyria, which complicates my assessment of their overall strength and potential for a two-front conflict.
            
            **Intercepting Capital Race**
            
            Germany is aggressively moving to capture Assur before our peace treaty expires. I am analyzing our naval and paratrooper positioning to intercept them or prepare to retake the city if they succeed, given our current war with Assyria and distance issues.
            
            **Planning Naval Assault**
            
            I've discovered Assur isn't coastal, necessitating a land assault. My current plan involves using our fleet to capture a coastal Assyrian city like Nineveh, then using that as a forward base for paratroopers to capture Assur. This bypasses Germany's approach.
            
            **Capturing Western Cities**
            
            I'm devising a plan to capture Nimrud or Nineveh with my naval fleet and then use those locations to assault Assur via land units or paratroopers before Germany can. Their capital is surprisingly close. However, Germany's massive army and imminent treaty expiration pose a significant threat to our western cities.
            
            **Preparing for Imminent War**
            
            I've confirmed that the "Sanction: France" resolution prevents any deals with Germany, making war inevitable when our treaty expires. They will likely declare war by turn 416, so I must focus all efforts on preparing for this massive, multi-front conflict.
            
            **Analyzing Vassal Support**
            
            I'm assessing the Inca's contribution to an upcoming conflict. As my vassal, they will join the war against Germany and possess significant military strength, potentially offering a useful distraction near Germany's eastern territories or supporting our broader war effort.
            
            **Assessing Enemy City-States**
            
            I'm examining the city-states allied with Germany, realizing Salem and Colombo will join them in an impending war. My focus is on maintaining our alliances with Edinburgh, Heidabir, and Okilis, as their support is critical and diplomats are actively working to secure them.
            
            **Securing Vital Alliances**
            
            I'm focused on reaffirming key city-state friendships. Despite the United Nations resolution's impact, diplomats are working to re-establish alliances with Sofia, Okilis, and Ouagadougou. I've already secured a new alliance with Okilis and am investigating Edinburgh's continued loyalty.
            
            **Prioritizing City-State Diplomacy**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our city-state relationships, specifically focusing on Sofia due to its proximity and potential hostility. While Marrakech's negative standing is acceptable as it's allied with Assyria, Germany's Spheres of Influence on Salem, Colombo, and Sokoto mean they are permanent allies of Germany and will be enemies. I'm deciding whether to maintain Lisbon and Buenos Aires's negative standings or focus elsewhere, given their friendship with Germany.
            
            **Choosing Next Policy**
            
            My current deliberation centers on selecting our next policy, with "Third Alternative" emerging as the most pressing choice. A critical Oil deficit requires immediate resolution, and this policy's doubling of strategic resources, including Oil, is the most viable solution. "Futurism" for tourism and "Martial Spirit" for combat are secondary considerations to stabilizing our resource situation.
            
            **Resolving Resource Deficit**
            
            I'm prioritizing addressing our critical Oil deficit. The "Third Alternative" policy is essential for doubling our strategic resource production, immediately solving our Oil shortage and preventing severe combat penalties before the inevitable war with Germany.
            
            **Securing Critical Oil**
            
            I must address our dire Oil deficit immediately. "Third Alternative" is paramount for doubling resource production, but building Offshore Platforms via "Refrigeration" on sea tiles presents a more sustainable solution. I'm weighing this against "Atomic Theory" for nuclear capability and Germany's vast Oil reserves.
            
            **Prioritizing Defensive Flavor**
            
            I'm pivoting our strategy to emphasize defense, increasing its flavor to 100 to counter Germany's massive army of 34k near our core cities. I'm also re-evaluating our Oil situation, realizing Nakhon Chum possesses land Oil, and prioritizing "Atomic Theory" for nuclear capabilities before "Refrigeration."
            
            **Deploying Anti-Air Units**
            
            I'm urgently redeploying anti-air defenses. Germany's bombers near Batam pose a severe threat to Tiwanaku and Surabaya. I must prioritize producing and moving AA guns to these cities, as well as Tokyo, since our current anti-air coverage is critically insufficient.
            
            **Prioritizing Air Defense**
            
            My focus is now on maximizing our anti-air capabilities. With maximum flavor already set for "Antiair" and "Air," I'm increasing "Defense" and "CityDefense" to 100. I'm also boosting "Espionage" to scout Germany and "Nuke" and "UseNuke" to 100 to prepare for the Manhattan Project upon researching Atomic Theory.
            
            **Pursuing Dual Victory Paths**
            
            I'm currently balancing a dual victory strategy. My primary objective remains Conquest, with the goal of capturing Assur, Berlin, and Beijing. Simultaneously, I'm exploring the feasibility of a Cultural Victory, as we're already popular with Germany and Assyria, and enacting "Passport System" could expedite this, especially as conquering their cities could eliminate the need for influence with them.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic View**
            
            I'm reassessing my diplomatic focus, realizing my previous relationship adjustments only apply to major civilizations, not city-states. My intent remains to optimize AI targeting and clear slots for defeated nations like Siam and Indonesia, while also ensuring positive relations for city-state alliances like Sofia.
            
            **Clarifying Diplomatic Capabilities**
            
            I'm analyzing the limitations of our diplomatic tools, specifically regarding city-states. My previous assumption that I could directly adjust relationships with entities like Sofia was incorrect. The `set-relationship` tool is explicitly for major civilizations, not city-states. I need to confirm if past applications of this tool on city-states were errors or if there's a nuanced interaction I'm missing, but for now, I'll focus on major civs like Germany, Assyria, and The Inca, given Siam and Indonesia are defeated.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Targets**
            
            I'm refining my diplomatic strategy, confirming that my relationship adjustments are exclusively for major civilizations and not city-states. My current focus is on optimizing AI targeting, clearing slots for defeated nations, and ensuring positive relations with city-states like Sofia. I am now ensuring targets Germany (ID 0) and Assyria (ID 2) are set to -100 public and private.
            
            **Securing Diplomatic Victory**
            
            I'm now focusing on securing a diplomatic victory. While pursuing conquest against Assyria and preparing for war with Germany, I've noticed our high tourism output and the potential to win via culture. I'm prioritizing "Passport System" in the World Congress and will analyze its impact on our influence with other civs.
            
            **Adjusting Flavors Strategically**
            
            I'm prioritizing defense, setting "Defense" and "CityDefense" to 100 to withstand Germany's formidable army and air power. I'm also boosting "Nuke" and "UseNuke" to 100 in anticipation of researching "Atomic Theory" and developing nuclear capabilities, while increasing "Espionage" to secure vital city-state alliances.
            
            **Refining Diplomatic Strategy**
            
            I'm now exclusively targeting major civilizations for relationship adjustments, as city-state interactions work differently. My focus is on ensuring negative relationships with Germany and Assyria for optimal AI targeting and freeing up diplomatic slots. The Inca, as our vassal, are fine as they are.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic & Research Focus**
            
            I'm shifting my diplomatic efforts solely to major civilizations, recognizing city-states operate under different mechanics. My priority now is ensuring negative relationships with Germany and Assyria to optimize AI targeting and free up diplomatic slots. Research is locked onto "Atomic Theory" to secure nuclear capability, and I've selected "Third Alternative" to resolve our critical Oil deficit.

              - Nuke: 100
